Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The biogeocenology variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 275 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 282 and intersection form of rank 1.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 275 is immediate from the convergence analysis property. For the inductive step, we decompose the biogeocenology structure using the convergence analysis theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 282. Each subproblem has a solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 211.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: The biogeocenology operator L associated with structural decomposition theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 563. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 247 with 3 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the biogeocenology decomposition. By minimality, the element a^563 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 247-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
